## Sunday, 9:07 a.m. – Day 1 Undefeated Players and Decks

*by Blake Rasmussen*

Six players found their way through the crowd without losing a match, paced by Gaudenis Vidugiris, Andy Probasco, and Trent Jones with pristine 9-0 records. Vidugiris rode a Mono Blue Merfolk and Wizards list to the top of the standings, followed by Probasco's Countertop deck and Jones' Goblins. Kurtis Droge, Paul Rietzl, and Adam Yurchick were also undefeated at 8-0-1. Yurchick protected his 10/10 with Countebalance, while Rietzl's Nearly Mono Black and Droge's Blue Zoo list propelled them to day two.

#### Gaudenis Vidugiris

## Round 10 Gaudenis Vidugiris vs. Andy Probasco

*by Blake Rasmussen*

Gaudenis Vidugiris and Andy Probasco are two of the three players remaining with no ties and no losses. Vidugiris is sporting a *Lorwyn* take on the old Fish archetype, supplemented by the Merfolk tribe. Vintage and Legacy specialist Andy Probasco was running Countertop and to great effect. Both players were 9-0 and sitting pretty heading into Day 2.

**Game 1**  
 Probasco kept his opener, while Vidugiris very quietly shipped his back. The reason for his quiet demeanor?

“I can't talk,” he managed in barely above a whisper.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/7c/70/7c708f75d14ce77e856f80e21db822d06da10605abf6facc3181c225e042e9ff.jpg)

idugiris' six was much better, and he had the first action of the game in a turn one [Æther Vial](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=%C3%86ther+Vial), which Probasco [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will)ed. He followed that up with a [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), already at three power.

Vidugiris played both [Æther Vial](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=%C3%86ther+Vial) and [Cursecatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cursecatcher) on his turn, both of which earned a quick “sure” from Probasco. This was quite possibly the quietest all-English-speaking feature match ever.

Probasco ran out both Top and [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) on his turn, growing his ‘Goyf and attacking for four and adding an [Academy Ruins](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Academy+Ruins) to the table. Vidugiris tried [Umezawa's Jitte](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Umezawa%27s+Jitte) on his turn, but Probasco had the [Spell Snare](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Snare), and was content to simply swing Vidugiris to 12 and pass the turn.

The following turn, Vidugiris tried to set up a [Standstill](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Standstill) with [Æther Vial](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Aether%2BVial) in play. In response, Probasco simply flipped his Top without checking and cast [Krosan Grip](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Krosan+Grip) on the [Æther Vial](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=%C3%86ther+Vial), leaving Vidugiris in the unenviable position of having to crack his own [Standstill](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Standstill) with [Silvergill Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silvergill+Adept) the following turn. The [Standstill](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Standstill) drew Probasco into a [Spell Snare](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Snare), stopping the 2/1 and allowing Probasco to swing in unabated once more and add a second [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) to the table.

When a [Silvergill Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silvergill+Adept) drew nothing of importance, Vidugiris moved on to Game 2.

Andy Probasco 1 Gaudenis Vidugiris 0

**Game 2**  
 Once again, both players shuffled up in near complete silence, which probably had as much to do with the hour as it did with Vidugiris' throat. The players noted the change in time as Rich Shay walked by and talked about his early round win...because his opponent didn't show up.

“I was terrified I would do that,” said Probasco, echoing the sentiments of many on turning their clocks forward overnight.

Back in non-farming-related action, Vidugiris once again mulliganed, while Probasco kept and led with [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top). Vidugiris had the turn two [Silvergill Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silvergill+Adept), revealing [Lord of Atlantis](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lord+of+Atlantis) to start his early clock.

The following turn the Lord was stopped by [Spell Snare](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Snare), but the Silvergill took Andrew down to 17 before being dwarfed by [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf). Probasco added [Umezawa's Jitte](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Umezawa%27s+Jitte) to his side the following turn, but simply passed back without equipping or attacking.

Probasco didn't hold back in the face of [Umezawa's Jitte](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Umezawa%27s+Jitte), however, and freely attacked with his [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf). When Vidugiris attempted to attack with a [Umezawa's Jitte](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Umezawa%27s+Jitte)-wielding Adept the following turn, Probasco was ready with [Ancient Grudge](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancient+Grudge), also growing his [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) in the process.

[Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) was met with [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) the following turn, and Probasco merely shrugged and attacked for five with [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf). Sometimes big green men just get there.

Vidugiris wasn't finished yet, though, as he resolved a [Merrow Reejerey](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Merrow+Reejerey) and attacked Probasco down to 10 while he sat at 12 from ‘Goyf attacks. The Reejerey meant the [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) couldn't even block effectively, as it risked getting tapped down prior to attackers.

Probasco Topped, sacrificed a land and Topped again, looking for an answer, which he found in [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout). Rather, almost found. The last two cards in Vidugiris' hand were [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) and blue card ([Standstill](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Standstill), if it really matters). Probasco instead attacked with his [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), knocking Vidugiris to 6.

In turn, Vidugiris cracked back with his entire team, then played [Cursecatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cursecatcher) and passed with Probasco down to three while he stood one point out of [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) range.

On his turn, Probasco simply cracked a fetch land, knocked the top of his deck, and Topped. Finding no help he conceded to a horde of Mer-people.

Andy Probasco 1 Gaudenis Vidugiris 1

**Game 3**  
 Both players kept this game, and Probasco kicked things off by [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder)ing his options and playing a [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance), while Vidugiris had the turn one [Æther Vial](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=%C3%86ther+Vial) and a [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland) on turn two for [Tropical Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tropical+Island).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/c3/0a/c30a986b19bd10f263faf3e2bb3be5a2104ef62a25604d67b7055574ecee2cf9.jpg)

Probasco then added [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top) to his board, while Vidugiris [Æther Vial](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Aether%2BVial)ed a [Cursecatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cursecatcher), completely ignoring the nearly broken countering engine. On his turn, Vidugiris attacked for one with the ‘Catcher and [Æther Vial](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Aether%2BVial)ed in a main phase [Silvergill Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silvergill+Adept).

Countertop in tow, Probasco attempted to shut down [Æther Vial](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=%C3%86ther+Vial) with [Ancient Grudge](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancient+Grudge), which resulted in a minor [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will)s. Probasco came out on top (no pun intended...[Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top) was not involved) , and Vidugiris was forced to bin his Countertop-resistant artifact.

His follow-up, [Merrow Reejerey](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Merrow+Reejerey), was pretty Countebalance-resistant as well, and allowed Vidugiris to attack for five, dropping Probasco to 11. [Sower of Temptation](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sower+of+Temptation) however, stole the Reejerey and Vidugiris' momentum.

Yet another Reejerey snuck through [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) (revealing [Ancient Grudge](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancient+Grudge)). Probasco had no play on his turn other than to Top and attack for two with [Sower of Temptation](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sower+of+Temptation).

Vidugiris, sensing the advantage, animated his [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) and attacked into his own Reejerey with [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault), Reejerey, [Silvergill Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silvergill+Adept), and [Cursecatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cursecatcher). Probasco chose not to trade Reejereys, instead he simply chump blocked the [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) and fell to four.

The reason became apparent the following turn, as [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) yielded at least a temporary solution, though it cost him four mana when [Cursecatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cursecatcher) jumped in the way and [Riptide Laboratory](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riptide+Laboratory) bounced [Silvergill Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silvergill+Adept).

Still, Probasco had to find a way to deal with a [Riptide Laboratory](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riptide+Laboratory)-backed [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault), and a [Merrow Reejerey](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Merrow+Reejerey) that resolved through [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance). [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) off the Top (in both senses of the word) was a pretty solid answer. So much so that Vidugiris was forced to simply pass the turn back.

Things got worse as Probasco added [Vedalken Shackles](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vedalken+Shackles) to the table, which for the first time all match led Vidugiris to cough out “That's not good.”

And it wasn't. Probasco stole [Merrow Reejerey](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Merrow+Reejerey) and was suddenly able to go on the offensive with [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), taking Vidugiris down to 12, who then passed his turn with no play. Probasco attacked once again and Vidugiris fell to 7. Curiously, he neglected to block with his [Riptide Laboratory](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riptide+Laboratory)-backed [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault).

When Probasco attempted a second [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), Vidugiris had to stem the bleeding, and tried to foul up Probasco's lock by targeting [Polluted Delta](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polluted+Delta) with [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland), which prompted Probasco to sacrifice it. Vidugiris then returned his [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite), and activated [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) to attempt to counter with Spellstutter. However, when Probasco's Top yielded another two-drop, Vidugiris packed it in.

Andy Probasco now sits at 10-0, poised to make a run at the Top 8.

Andy Probasco defeats Gaudenis Vidugiris 2-1

  

## Sunday, 9:39 – Talking with Head Judge Jason Ness

*by Bill Stark*

It is an often thankless job, yet a vital one to the successful completion of any premier **Magic** event. The Head Judge; overseer, supreme ruler, final say on all matters. The coverage team sat down with Level IV judge Jason Ness early Sunday morning to ask him what heading the largest North American Grand Prix in the history of the game was like, as well as any interesting challenges posed by the unique Legacy format.

“There were some interesting card interactions,” Jason began, quickly detailing one particular match involving the *Alliances* artifact Helm of Obedience and [Leyline of the Void](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Leyline+of+the+Void). “The Oracle ruling on Helm directs a player to put cards into their graveyard until they meet one of two conditions. Since Leyline replaces the effect of cards going into the graveyard, those conditions are never met and you simply deck someone.” Ness had seen the play happen at least once during Saturday's portion of competition.

There were three specific cards that had accounted for a significant portion of confusion from players. “[Lion's Eye Diamond](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lion%27s+Eye+Diamond) tricks a lot of people. Because it has the text ‘play this ability as an instant' many players think they can use [Stifle](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stifle) effects to counter it; you can't.” Jason quickly added another thing you couldn't do with the card “You can't use L.E.D. to play a spell in your hand.”

The other two-thirds of the holy triumvirate of Legacy befuddlement? [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) and [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top), particularly in conjunction with one another. “I was shocked at the amount of people who tried to activate [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top) without actually having one in play,” Jason explained. Most of the time it was simply a gaffe, from players so used to having the artifact on the board they took for granted it might not be. Many others activated it after using it to draw card, forgetting for the moment it was on the top of their deck instead of in play. “[Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) was confusing too, with players unsure whether revealing a land to [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) could counter a zero casting cost spell.” It does. He also detailed a mistaken ruling from the feature match area on Saturday, where a judge errantly ruled that if a player didn't consciously make mention of triggering the [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) before doing something in response (like activating [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top) to arrange the top three cards of the library favorably), the trigger was missed and the player couldn't reveal. “Whenever an opponent plays a spell, [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance)*must* trigger. When it comes to revealing the card, the controller of [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) may *choose* to do that.” Response or not, the [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) trigger goes on the stack.

The conversation turned from specific card interactions to the details of handling the largest Grand Prix on the record books for North America. “I was really impressed with the round turnover time, despite the massive amount of players,” Ness exclaimed. It's certainly something that's easy to take for granted, but Jason pointed out that because judges must verify a match slip result and pick them up from the table when a match finishes, unlike at many smaller events. Jason's judge staff could very well spend much of their time in a round rushing around 1,200+ players picking up match slips. “The logistics are certainly challenging.”

We're on our way to a smooth finish on Sunday, and it's in large part thanks to the cool headed handling of head judge Jason Ness and his talented judge staff.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/56/ee/56ee0f1f6e725881e966473b033c449b8795b884ee58f346abb30d6b5c3b0ab2.jpg)*Head Judge Jason Ness.*  

## Round 11: Kurtis Droge vs. Gerry Thompson

*by Bill Stark*

“Where you from?” Gerry Thompson asked his opponent Kurtis Droge as they sat down to their Round 11 Feature Match.

“Ann Arbor, Michigan.” Droge explained. The two chatted amiably as they prepared for the go-ahead to begin the round. Gerry Thompson has become a household name in America of late, working with some of the game's brightest minds and becoming a respected writer. While Kurtis was new to the Feature Match scene, he outlined some of the communities he played with in Michigan.

Gerry won the die roll and opened on a [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top), but the real action started when Kurtis attempted a [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) on his second turn. Thompson responded by using a sac land to search up [Tropical Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tropical+Island), activating his Top, then allowing the ‘Goyf to resolve. He untapped, then attempted a [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) of his own, which was met with [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) from Droge. That merited a Force back from Gerry, and the *Future Sight* standout hit the board.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/20/c5/20c59b572318f3dae7067e6f93bb20f7458fd9dd574ec19f0a429791c087325a.jpg)*Kurtis Droge makes his debut in the Feature Match area.*

A third copy of the creature resolved a turn later when Kurtis played his second copy of the match, but when Gerry tried to make it a whopping *four* [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf)s, Droge had a [Spell Snare](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Snare) to successfully prevent Thompson from sticking the creature. Gerry began working his Top in an effort to gain an advantage while being behind, but Droge had double [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) to keep himself in the card drawing game.

[Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares) found its way to Gerry's hand, and he used the uber instant to RFG one of the opposing Tarmogoys, evening the ‘Goyf count to one per player. With a (now) mostly even board, Thompson had given himself a small virtual lead thanks to his Divining Top, but Droge remained dug in. He ripped a [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) that he combined with a sac land to refresh his hand, then played a [Wild Nacatl](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Nacatl) and [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape). All of a sudden his deck had morphed from the standard [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) deck that had seen much play in the Feature Match area over the weekend to a controlling Zoo deck!

Gerry made an effort to keep up with all the creatures, playing a [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant). Droge revealed the final card in his hand, a [Spell Snare](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Snare), to counter, and Gerry tried to come up with a counter-counter using Divining Top. Failing to do so, he passed the turn and was immediately attacked with all of Droge's team. Gerry's [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) munched the [Wild Nacatl](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Nacatl), but he fell to 3. Kurtis played the card he had drawn that turn, a replacement [Wild Nacatl](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Nacatl), and passed. Thompson needed some help to survive the next combat step, but he had plenty of opportunities.

He used Top to rearrange the top portion of his deck, then cashed the artifact in to draw a card. The card in question was [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant), a valiant chump blocker that could help Gerry survive the turn. Unfortunately for him, the 2/1 could also spell his demise as he had his [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top) on the top of his library, guaranteeing he would take a point during his next turn should Droge opt not to attack. From there, Thompson would have to hit some miraculous spins from the Top, hitting a land throughout the rest of the game so he could reveal a pain free draw to [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant), or risk losing to his very own mercy blocker.

Droge rendered the discussion moot on his draw step, however, peeling Fire/Ice and pointing the burn spell at both [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant) and Gerry's head, clearing the way for a lethal attack.

Kurtis Droge 1, Gerry Thompson 0

The two players sideboarded in silence for their second game, neither letting on what changes to their maindeck they were making. Droge's deck posed an interesting take on the Threshold archetype, with a Zoo shell few players could be seen playing on Saturday. The aggressive plan had worked well for him in the first game, though he had been the beneficiary of some fortunate draws in the mid game.

Thompson kicked off the second game with a [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top), but when Droge made a [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape) on his first turn, the two players dropped themselves to zero lands on matching [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze)s. That meant the Ape resolved, and it was the 1/1 staring down Gerry's Top. Thompson played [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland) and passed the turn.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/85/bf/85bffe98e6390e9feb9092fb4a887ba3d0703d821e696ff3e0f2f75506abdf61.jpg)*Grand Prix powerhouse Gerry Thompson.*

[Vedalken Shackles](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vedalken+Shackles) was the next major spell on the table, coming to the defense of Gerry Thompson or at least attempting to do so. Kurtis revealed a second [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) to counter the spell, then dropped a [Wild Nacatl](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Nacatl) to increase the size of his red zone team. Thompson played a second [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland) and, with Droge on just a [Volcanic Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Island) and a [Tropical Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tropical+Island) for lands, threatened to significantly reduce the size of Droge's army should he choose to eat said lands with the non-basic hate. [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares) came forth to nail the Nacatl, and Gerry fell to 13 from a 1/1 [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape) attack. The board had stalled significantly for Kurtis Droge, but he still had a good chunk of cards in hand to try to get something going.

Looking to prevent exactly that, Gerry Thompson spent his turn using [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland) to hit Kurtis' only untapped land, the [Volcanic Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Island), then cashed in his Divining Top for a card. That let him play [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance), and he passed the turn. Droge attacked for 2 after playing a [Savannah](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Savannah), then dropped [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf). The green powerhouse soon switched sides of the table, however, as Thompson untapped and played [Sower of Temptation](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sower+of+Temptation). That left Gerry with [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), Sower, and [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) with [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top). The game was firmly getting out of hand for Kurtis Droge, and he was aware.

[Krosan Grip](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Krosan+Grip) allowed Kurtis to dispose of his opponent's [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance), but he fell to 14 life when Gerry attacked him with his own [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) and the [Sower of Temptation](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sower+of+Temptation) that had stolen it. When Gerry tried to make the same attack a turn later, Kurtis used [Red Elemental Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Red+Elemental+Blast) to blow up the 2/2, getting his ‘Goyf back. He didn't have it for long, however, as Gerry played a second Sower post-combat to re-steal the [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf).

Unable to get his original [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) back, Droge just added another to the table, which resolved successfully. He then attacked Gerry to 7 with [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape). Thompson attacked back with Sower, putting Kurtis to 11 and played a [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) of his own, winning the ensuing counter battle. It was a rough turn of events for Kurtis, but he got some help from the top of his deck in the form of [Ranger of Eos](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ranger+of+Eos), searching up two [Wild Nacatl](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Nacatl)s and ensuring he would have plenty of gas for the near future.

The back and forth continued as Gerry responded to his opponent's fortunate series of plays by furiously spinning his Top, finding another [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), and hopping it into play. That gave him a board of triple [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) and a [Sower of Temptation](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sower+of+Temptation), while Droge's board became double Nacatl, [Ranger of Eos](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ranger+of+Eos), [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape), and [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf). Sensing an opening, Gerry turned his entire team sideways forcing Kurtis to figure out blocks in order to survive the turn. When all was said and done, Droge was at 1 and had lost just a [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape). Gerry played a post-combat [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant) to chump, threatening lethal the following turn.

Droge clearly had a plan as he took the turn, excitedly untapping. He revealed a Fire/Ice to deal 1 to [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant) and 1 to Thompson, clearing the way for his team. Gerry didn't have the counter, the Fire resolved, and Kurtis bashed with lethal, clinching the match in a sweep despite the fact both games had felt shifted to Gerry Thompson's favor.

Kurtis Droge 2, Gerry Thompson 0

  

## Sunday, 10:45 a.m. - Dealer Talk

*by Blake Rasmussen*

Dealers tend to have a unique finger on the pulse of any given tournament. For one, it's their job to know what cards players will and will not buy. They quite literally pay their rent based on how well they know what cards to lug all the way to large tournaments. Conversely, on the day of, dealers get a front-row seat to the metagame as they sell players their last-minute deck-fillers.

 

![Humility](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Humility)

This weekend, with Legacy taking the stage, a number of players were looking for older, harder to find cards. Cards that haven't seen a PTQ in years, if ever, came out in force as dealers sold [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland), Sinkhole, [Null Rod](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Null+Rod), [Misdirection](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Misdirection), [Back to Basics](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Back+to+Basics), Rishidan Port, [Vindicate](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vindicate), [Humility](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Humility), and [Moat](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Moat) were all strong sellers on the weekend.

[Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance)/Top drove much of the sales this weekend as well, as its two namesakes sold well across the board and undoubtedly helped drive up demand for counterparts like [Stifle](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stifle), [Spell Snare](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Snare), and [Engineered Explosives](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Engineered+Explosives). Likewise, cards like [Krosan Grip](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Krosan+Grip) and [Trygon Predator](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Trygon+Predator) were popular for their ability to fight through the soft lock.

Some staples, new and old, sold well too. [Kataki, War's Wage](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kataki%2C+War%27s+Wage), [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile), [Terravore](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terravore), [Magus of the Moon](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Magus+of+the+Moon), [Chalice of the Void](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chalice+of+the+Void), and the various fetch lands and dual lands all sold well.

 

![Natural Order](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Natural+Order)

The talk of the tournament prior to this weekend was the interaction between [Natural Order](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Natural+Order) and [Progenitus](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Progenitus). While a number of players opted to run the “combo,” dealers reported mostly lukewarm sales. [Progenitus](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Progenitus) was mostly a one-of in those decks, so soft sales there weren't particularly surprising, but dealers also reported disappointing sales of [Natural Order](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Natural+Order).

A number of other cards performed below general expectations. Cards from both burn and combo decks were below what most dealers thought, and [Lion's Eye Diamond](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lion%27s+Eye+Diamond) was nearly unanimously the most disappointing. Other combo cards like [Tendrils of Agony](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tendrils+of+Agony) were similarly under-purchased. And while [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) raised all cards with it, it seemed players were eschewing the [Phyrexian Dreadnaught](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Phyrexian+Dreadnaught+) kill. Likewise, [Grindstone](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grindstone) and [Painter's Servant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Painter%27s+Servant) may be exciting, but not enough to garner much attention this weekend.

 

![Circle of Protection: Green](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Circle+of+Protection%3A+Green)

Overall, the weekend held few surprises on the dealers' end, but a few requests took them by surprise. Players scrambled for answers to the protection-from-everything [Progenitus](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Progenitus), and came up with some long-forgotten answers. [Perish](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Perish), [Hibernation](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hibernation), and [Circle of Protection: Green](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Circle+of+Protection%3A+Green) were among the most popular, as each had the added advantage of answering [Nimble Mongoose](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nimble+Mongoose), [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), and the occasional [Trygon Predator](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Trygon+Predator)

## Round 12 - Brian Kowal vs. Reuben Bresler

*by Blake Rasmussen*

“How do I know you, you look familiar?” asked Brian Kowal as he sat down across from Reuben Bresler.  
 “I wrote for Starcity Games for 30 seconds. I made stupid videos,” offered Bresler amongst the chatter. “Oh, and I won a PTQ once.”  
 “Are we friends on Facebook?” asked Kowal.  
 “Yeah, that's probably it. My biggest Magic achievement is convincing people I was good enough to hire.”

Getting recognized by Brian Kowal? Priceless. Getting recognized by Brian Kowal because you're possibly unknowingly Facebook friends? Slightly less priceless.

Kowal and Bresler had both brought decks aimed squarely at [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) decks, and had been paid off as both were within reach of the Top 8. Kowal was packing discard spells backed by [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant), [Tombstalker](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tombstalker), and [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf). Bresler meanwhile had “12 main deck [Vindicate](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vindicate)s” with [Red Elemental Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Red+Elemental+Blast), [Pyroblast](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroblast), and the often forgotten Ancient Volcano, all backed by everyone's favorite 1/3 [Painter's Servant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Painter%27s+Servant) (sorry, [Bottle Gnomes](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bottle+Gnomes)).

Each showed their proclivity for unusual die rolls, with Kowal opting for even/odd and Bresler recounting, well: “I played Monopoly rules earlier. High roll, but re-roll on doubles and if you roll three doubles in a row, you go to jail, er, lose. It was a blast.”

![](https://i.ormos.uk/62/07/6207a1dd8ece855443217431157f551eb27bb063d99eefa4e182cf3a281ae5df.jpg)*Ladies and gentlemen, Reuben Bresler.*

**Game 1**  
 Kowal, on the play, kept, while Bresler led with [Ancient Tomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancient+Tomb) and a discarded [Simian Spirit Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Simian+Spirit+Guide) for [Magus of the Moon](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Magus+of+the+Moon). Kowal responded and searched up a basic [Swamp](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swamp).

The next turn he used that [Swamp](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swamp) to [Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Ritual) and [Mesmeric Fiend](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mesmeric+Fiend), seeing Active Volcano, [Grindstone](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grindstone), [Gamble](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gamble), [Pyroblast](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroblast) and [City of Traitors](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=City+of+Traitors). Kowal took the [Grindstone](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grindstone), the cast [Hymn to Tourach](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hymn+to+Tourach), hitting the [Pyroblast](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroblast) and Active Volcano.

After attacking with [Magus of the Moon](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Magus+of+the+Moon), Bresler cast [Gamble](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gamble), discarding [City of Traitors](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=City+of+Traitors) and passing with no other play.

Both players attacked with their utility dorks for a few turns while Kowal made more “mountains” and Bresler managed a [Grindstone](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grindstone) with no third mana or [Painter's Servant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Painter%27s+Servant). Kowal made a [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant) while Bresler [Gamble](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gamble)d once again.

“This deck's sweet,” said Kowal in the face of a second [Gamble](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gamble). This time, the [Gamble](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gamble) didn't pay off, as Bresler was forced to randomly discard the [Painter's Servant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Painter%27s+Servant). His fake crying didn't seem to move Kowal much.

Post-[Gamble](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gamble), Bresler ran out a second [Magus of the Moon](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Magus+of+the+Moon) off of [Chrome Mox](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chrome+Mox). Kowal one-uped him by [Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Ritual)ing out a [Tombstalker](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tombstalker).

“Hm, that's more than a little awkward,” said Bresler right before randomly activating [Grindstone](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grindstone), which milled only two. It didn't matter, as [Tombstalker](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tombstalker) was too fat and too soon for Bresler's combo.

Brian Kowal 1 Reuben Bresler 0

**Game 2**  
![](https://i.ormos.uk/ca/07/ca0751b825fc0de5860ada723f0e2fc3b09c662b42b8f1b39435dd2fe5f74207.jpg) ”Now I bring in my whole sideboard,” threatened Kowal. “I was prepared for Painter.”  
 “You're the only person in the room then,” said the nearly undefeated Bresler.  
 “What'd you lose to?”  
 “An awkward Red White aggro deck.”  
 “Well, Red White beats everything,” said the inventor of the Boat Brew.

Kowal's turn-one [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) revealed [Pyroblast](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroblast), [Red Elemental Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Red+Elemental+Blast), and [Imperial Recruiter](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Imperial+Recruiter), plus two lands. Somehow this resulted in Bresler singing “People let me tell ‘ya ‘bout my best friend.” For those who know Bresler, this isn't terribly unusual.

Bresler dropped the [Imperial Recruiter](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Imperial+Recruiter) on the table, fetching [Goblin Welder](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Welder).

Kowal returned serve on his turn by fetching out another basic land and playing [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant). He declined to block with it the following turn, as the Recruiter crashed in unimpeded. [Painter's Servant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Painter%27s+Servant), naming blue, and the Welder soon followed. A [Mesmeric Fiend](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mesmeric+Fiend) revealed just how good the servant was, as Bresler revealed two [Red Elemental Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Red+Elemental+Blast)s and a [Pyroblast](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroblast).

One turn later, Kowal had no creatures on board and Bresler had no cards in hand. Kowal, on the other hand, had plenty. A [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares) removed the [Painter's Servant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Painter%27s+Servant), and soon a [Tombstalker](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tombstalker) threatened to take over.

Kowal found another Imperial Recruiter, and fetched a [Painter's Servant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Painter%27s+Servant), needing only a [Grindstone](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grindstone) and an unimpeded turn to go off. However, when Kowal played a second [Tombstalker](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tombstalker) and Bresler drew blanks, the superiority of Tombstalkers over Imperial Recruiters was confirmed.

Brian Kowal defeats Reuben Bresler 2-0

  

## Sunday, 11:48 a.m. - Quick Hits: What's the Best Two-Drop in Legacy?

*by Bill Stark*

![](https://i.ormos.uk/cb/f4/cbf4a5d18bbbcc1e222b82c956495571c33dba64f8d145718706fb24adbf11d2.jpg)*Luis Scott-Vargas*  
 Luis Scott-Vargas: [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance)

![](https://i.ormos.uk/79/5c/795c565c207750fb786af241b8c1e88afa41fa5a8efa655316d31850ccec2ed5.jpg)*Richard Feldman*  
 Richard Feldman: [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance)?

![](https://i.ormos.uk/2a/0b/2a0ba3f7da4f260f6b425a5cd9461bc90cea7b5c306bb143d18a341c85cc2987.jpg)*Carlos Romao*
