## Sunday, 12:35 p.m. - Day 2 Metagame Breakdown

*by Duncan McGregor*

<table><tbody><tr><td>Type</td>&#10;			<td>Total</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>CounterTop</td>&#10;			<td>19</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Goblins</td>&#10;			<td>17</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>ANT</td>&#10;			<td>10</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Merfolk</td>&#10;			<td>10</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Threshold</td>&#10;			<td>8</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Dreadstill</td>&#10;			<td>7</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Landstill</td>&#10;			<td>6</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Aggro Loam</td>&#10;			<td>6</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>BG(w) aggro</td>&#10;			<td>5</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Dredge</td>&#10;			<td>5</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>RG aggro</td>&#10;			<td>5</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Mono-red aggro</td>&#10;			<td>4</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>4c control</td>&#10;			<td>2</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Affinity</td>&#10;			<td>2</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Combo Elves</td>&#10;			<td>2</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Natural Order Rock</td>&#10;			<td>2</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Painter's Servant / Grindstone</td>&#10;			<td>2</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Survival</td>&#10;			<td>2</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Team America</td>&#10;			<td>2</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>UW control</td>&#10;			<td>2</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>41 Land</td>&#10;			<td>1</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>4c aggro-control</td>&#10;			<td>1</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Belcher</td>&#10;			<td>1</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Countersliver</td>&#10;			<td>1</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>CounterTop / Dreadnought</td>&#10;			<td>1</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Dragon Stompy</td>&#10;			<td>1</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Faeries</td>&#10;			<td>1</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Mono-blue aggro</td>&#10;			<td>1</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>RG creatureless Loam</td>&#10;			<td>1</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>RGW aggro</td>&#10;			<td>1</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>Salvagers combo</td>&#10;			<td>1</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>UB control</td>&#10;			<td>1</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>UG aggro</td>&#10;			<td>1</td>&#10;		</tr><tr><td>UGB Standstill</td>&#10;			<td>1</td>&#10;		</tr></tbody></table>

The diverse Day 1 metagame that the Legacy card pool allowed has, at least partially, resolved itself. While the fight for the top continues, we can now look at the field to see the gauntlet that the remaining 132 players will have to face.

Leading the pack is a collection of 19 CounterTop aggro decks. These have their origin in the multicolored Threshold decks that are an established part of the Legacy metagame, but have largely moved away from threshold itself, removing the [Nimble Mongoose](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nimble+Mongoose)s that were the last remnant of that mechanic, with other cuts made as well. Replacing them are some combination of [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant)s for card advantage, [Krosan Grip](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Krosan+Grip)s for utility, [Vedalken Shackles](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vedalken+Shackles) or [Sower of Temptation](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sower+of+Temptation) for creature control, and additional land to power all that. The omnipresent [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) applies beatdown.

Following those in popularity is Goblins. A full 17 players rode the little red tribe into Day 2, using a combination of explosiveness, card advantage, and tutors to overwhelm their opponent. Splashes for utility cards vary, with black mana for [Warren Weirding](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Warren+Weirding)s and sometimes [Earwig Squad](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Earwig+Squad)s being common, and green for [Krosan Grip](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Krosan+Grip) and Tin-Street Hooligan also present.

Two decks each got 10 players over the cut. ANT - Ad Nauseum Tendrils - continues to work around the lack of [Mind's Desire](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mind%27s+Desire) to allow a viable storm deck in Legacy. Getting a proper storm count takes a bit more work, but the rewards are there for those willing to fight for them. On the other end of the spectrum, Merfolk's aggro-control strategy has also proved successful against the largely blue field. The combination of a reasonable clock and some of the best disruption available makes them very successful against some decks, and while they have very serious problems against others, the metagame gamble has paid off for many.

Moving into single digits, there are 8 surviving players piloting standard Threshold decks. Eschewing the [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance)/[Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top) synergy in favor of cards with more immediate impact, these use the same strategy as Merfolk: disruption + clock = win. The extra cards vary greatly, with burn, additional counters, or the [Natural Order](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Natural+Order) / [Progenitus](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Progenitus) combo all being represented.

Seven players are running [Dread](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dread)still - a blue-based control deck that uses [Stifle](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stifle)s and [Trickbind](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Trickbind) to trick Phyrexian [Dread](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dread)nought into play. Once they decide it's time to win, they don't want to waste any time. The remainder of the deck runs to card draw and counters, with black and green splashes both being seen. The similar Landstill deck, with 6 players, also aims to slow the game down with [Standstill](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Standstill), but uses a more gradual win condition, with Mishra's Factories and other creature-lands being the intended path to victory. They are typically blue-white, but as with other decks in Legacy, other colors can easily be splashed. The other Day 2 representative with 6 players is Aggro Loam. [Life from the Loam](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Life+from+the+Loam) is used to fuel [Countryside Crusher](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Countryside+Crusher), [Devastating Dreams](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Devastating+Dreams), and [Seismic Assault](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seismic+Assault), with [Terravore](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terravore) and [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) providing additional beatings.

Moving down the list, three decks each got 5 players over the cut. Black-Green Aggro decks use hand disruption and creature kill instead of counterspells to keep their opponents off balance until they can deliver a finishing blow. Usually splashing white as well for the efficient [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares), the attacks are supplied by [Tombstalker](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tombstalker)s and (again) [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf)s. [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) also figured in the red-green aggro decks, with the simple plan of “attack and burn.” In some cases, the ‘Goyf was the only green card in the main deck - after all, green doesn't usually go directly to the face. An additional four players actually went with that route - no green at all, just red cards for as quick a clock as they could get. Finally, the bogeyman of the graveyard, Dredge, is the last archetype with 5 representatives. Since Dredge's game plan is so different from every other deck in the format, it can steal wins from opponents who have no plan against it, but conversely prepared opponents can shut down the graveyard and deny it the one resource it truly needs. So far the gamble is paying off, at least for some.

While these are the most popular decks, there is no guarantee that the winner will be from one of these. A whopping 30 players piloted an additional 22 archetypes into Day 2, from [Painter's Servant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Painter%27s+Servant)/[Grindstone](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grindstone) combo to Dragon Stompy to Faeries. The fight for the top promises to be an interesting one.

  

## Round 13 Feature Match: Paul Rietzl vs. Luis Scott-Vargas

*by Bill Stark*

“I think the last time I played you, you had [Canopy Crawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Canopy+Crawler) in your deck.” Luis Scott-Vargas joked with his opponent as they sat down in the feature match area.

“What?” A befuddled Rietzl asked.

“It was a **Magic Online** *Onslaught/Legions/Scourge* Draft.” Luis clarified.

Rietzl was overjoyed to win a die roll, one of the few he claimed to have been victorious with on the weekend then kicked the match off with a [Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Ritual)-fueled super turn. The first [Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Dark%2BRitual) netted him [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant), and with the spare black mana he played a second [Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Dark%2BRitual), just enough for [Hypnotic Specter](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hypnotic+Specter).

“That's...going to be difficult.” Luis deadpanned.

Still, LSV isn't a super-pro for nothing. He had a [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares) to nail the 2/2 [Hypnotic Specter](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Hypnotic%2BSpecter), but was a bit dismayed to see Paul's second land drop in [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland). When Paul immediately used it on Luis' [Tundra](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tundra), it became clear why the Pro Tour-Kyoto finalist was so dismayed; the dual land was his only source of mana, and he passed the turn with an empty board. Paul's meager clock of [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant) went on the warpath, and he used a [Hymn to Tourach](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hymn+to+Tourach) to keep Scott-Vargas further off balance.

Luis finally found a [Polluted Delta](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polluted+Delta), which fetched [Tundra](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tundra), then set up his next few draw steps with a [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder). Rietzl's Bob charged in to put Luis at 12, then Paul played [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) seeing triple [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze), double [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance).

“Innnnnnteresting...” Paul said, before binning one of the [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze)s.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/a7/6f/a76ffc3ad7ba6882def707d5f309029f90564b8afdd1ed8d029fe9a177b12a40.jpg)*Luis Scott-Vargas maneuvers against a plethora of Dark Rituals.*

Luis ripped a [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares) off the top from his [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder), but was still stuck on one land. Paul was having difficulty finding pressure, however, playing an [Umezawa's Jitte](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Umezawa%27s+Jitte) but unable to get an attacker to carry the pointy stick. Luis found himself a [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top), then a second source of blue to play [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance). All of a sudden, Paul Rietzl was in trouble. Luis had established his deck's soft lock, and Paul needed to find a threat he could stick quickly or risk falling out of the game.

Meanwhile, Luis was busy trying to spin his way into an improbable comeback. He worked his Top, finding a [Flooded Strand](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flooded+Strand) to take him to three mana, and searched up an [Underground Sea](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Underground+Sea) that enabled him to play [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant). Paul's hand was growing larger and larger, but featured [Snuff Out](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snuff+Out)s, [Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Ritual)s, and Sinkholes, few of which were relevant at the moment. What he really needed was a creature that could resolve through [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance).

Paul played [Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Ritual), which was allowed to resolve. He used two of the mana to play Sinkhole, which merited a [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) from Luis, and the Sinkhole was countered as Scott-Vargas revealed a [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) on top of his deck to the [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) in play. “Burn for 1,” Paul said, failing to spend the last mana in his pool.

A second [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant) joined the team for LSV, and Paul Rietzl found himself at 13 life against an opponent with [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top) and [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance), drawing three cards a turn! Unbelievably, Luis was turning the game around in monstrous fashion and, a few turns later, he landed [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf). That let him send his entire team into the red zone and Luis Scott-Vargas had done it!

“I never drew a creature...” Paul lamented as he moved to his sideboard.

Luis Scott-Vargas 1, Paul Rietzl 0

Rietzl started the second game off by sacrificing his [Polluted Delta](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polluted+Delta) on his main phase to fetch up a [Bayou](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bayou). He failed to play any spells on the turn, instead passing. Cracking the sac land was a heads up play, however. He lost nothing doing so, and potentially played around an off-chance [Stifle](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stifle) from his opponent.

Paul's second play was [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant), and he passed the turn to Luis. The Pro Tour-Berlin winner dropped [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top), then went into the tank. Clearly missing a second land drop, he heavily considered blind drawing with his Top on the off chance he could hit a land. “Go for it!” Paul teased the California pro.

Luis did exactly that, masterfully ripping a [Tropical Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tropical+Island), then playing a *second* Top from his hand. “You're the best...” Rietzl sighed before attacking with his Confidant. A turn later, Paul's 2/1 was joined by a matching copy from Luis, but Scott-Vargas was going to need the help as Paul used two [Hymn to Tourach](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hymn+to+Tourach)s to knock Luis' hand down to just one card. A [Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top) was waiting for Luis, however, and Paul needed to find a way to continue putting pressure on his opponent.

A [Hypnotic Specter](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hypnotic+Specter) joined the Paul Rietzl side of the table, and he attacked to put the totals 12-10 in Luis Scott-Vargas' favor. To his credit, Luis managed to assemble the [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance)/Top combo, but lost his [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant) to a Rietzl [Diabolic Edict](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diabolic+Edict) and fell to 6 on Paul's attack. There was an outside hope for Luis, however, as Paul had fallen to 8. Because the East Coast player had [Tombstalker](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tombstalker) in his deck, it was possible he could reveal the massive creature to [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant), taking lethal. His next reveal was...[Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland). He breathed a sigh of relief, then laughed as his draw for the turn was [Tombstalker](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tombstalker). Had the cards been reversed, he would have lost the match on the spot!

Instead, his Confidant and Specter were able to hold the field of battle, clinching the second game and sending the players to a third.

Luis Scott-Vargas 1, Paul Rietzl 1

Luis Scott-Vargas started the rubber game with an [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island), while Rietzl came right out of the gates again with a [Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Ritual). His opponent nodded, and the mana jump resolved. That led to a Sinkhole targeting Luis' [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island), then a second [Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Ritual) got Paul [Nantuko Shade](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nantuko+Shade) and [Pithing Needle](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pithing+Needle). It was an amazing start again for Rietzl, but we had already seen such a beginning fail him earlier in the match.

A turn later he played [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland), blowing up Luis' second land drop of [Tundra](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tundra), then used [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) to see that Luis had no more lands and a hand full of expensive spells. The [Nantuko Shade](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nantuko+Shade) went to the red zone repeatedly, brutalizing Scott-Vargas' life total to 9. Fortunately Luis managed to draw back-to-back lands, allowing him a [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant), but the 2/1 was quickly dispatched by [Diabolic Edict](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diabolic+Edict) from Paul. Luis fell to 6.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/2d/d6/2dd61db0fb39b794980b88e52ca28d2056850db25b3cad26862c28c840e289da.jpg)*Paul Rietzl takes on one of the best.*

A turn later [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) came off the top for Luis, and Paul responded animatedly. “Oh *please* miss!” He exclaimed, praying for no [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares) for his opponent. Paul's deck had given him a phenomenal opening series of plays, but he had petered out after that and if Luis managed to stabilize, it would be all but over. No Swords was forthcoming, but Luis did find a land to let him play a second [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant) to chump.

Rietzl's Insect Shade wasn't afraid, charging into the Invitational card, but a [Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress) revealed Luis had only lands in his hand. Another [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant) came off the top to buy Luis a turn, but Paul had a [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland) to keep his opponent at two mana. That mana threshold was key as Luis had two copies of [Sower of Temptation](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sower+of+Temptation) in his hand. When Scott-Vargas failed to find a [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares) and couldn't actually play any cards in his hand, he was dead on the board.

Paul Rietzl 2, Luis Scott-Vargas 1

  

## Sunday, 12:45 p.m. - Rogue's Gallery

*by Blake Rasmussen*

While the weekend is shaping up to be a battle of Goblins and [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance)s, a number of players have chosen some more...interesting...routes to day two, including a few who find themselves in shouting distance of the Top 8.

Kurtis Droge is once such player, running a sort of Blue Zoo that might just be the only deck in the room running a combo you can find in *Shards of Alara* limited. In fact, Droge may be running more *Shards of Alara/Conflux* cards than any other player in the room between [Wild Nacatl](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Nacatl), [Ranger of Eos](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ranger+of+Eos), and [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile). A series of blue spells like [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze), [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will), Fire/Ice and [Spell Snare](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Snare) can keep his opponent off balance while he attacks with some of the most efficient creatures ever printed.



James Mink isn't the only person shooting for the (Magus of the) Moon on turn one, but is the only one running the full-on Dragon Stompy archetype on day two, complete with [Rakdos Pit Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Pit+Dragon) and a newer “dragon,” [Taurean Mauler](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Taurean+Mauler). Mauler is especially impressive where an opponent's turn can involve [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) into Sensei's Dining Top into [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) into [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will). After round 12 Mink was 10-2 and still in position to make the Top 8. [Chalice of the Void](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chalice+of+the+Void), [Trinisphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Trinisphere), and both moon effects give the widespread blue decks fits, and many of the deck's spells cost more than two, making it a solid choice in a [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) field.



Chris Andersen found his way to an 8-3-1 record after 12 rounds as the sole surviving 41-land deck. Circumventing the normal one-land-per-turn rule with explosive options like [Exploration](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Exploration) and Mana Bond, and fitting everything together with [Life from the Loam](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Life+from+the+Loam), Anderson can often lay his entire hand on the table turn one, and then draw multiple cards on turn two with [Life from the Loam](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Life+from+the+Loam) and cycling lands. Man lands provide the offense while Maze of Ith and Glacial Chasm play defense. Add to that the fact that his lands are immune to [Counterbalance](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Counterbalance) and his [Life from the Loam](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Life+from+the+Loam) outpaces [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland), and you've got a strong metagame contender.

#### Chris Andersen

Our last list fell short come Day 2, but as a combo aficionado I couldn't help but point out that Frank Emmert made it through Day 1 with Salvager's Combo and 61 cards. The deck looks get an [Auriok Salvagers](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Auriok+Salvagers) in play, then generate infinite mana with [Lion's Eye Diamond](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lion%27s+Eye+Diamond). From there, [Chromatic Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chromatic+Sphere) can draw the library to find [Pyrite Spellbomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyrite+Spellbomb) to deal as much damage as needed. The deck is a very cool sometimes contender that falls prey to splash damage on graveyard hate and from [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares) and [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile). It does have the potential to take some unaware players by surprise.

## Sunday, 1:35 p.m. - Public Events at Grand Prix-Chicago

*by Duncan McGregor*

While 1,230 players playing Legacy is, by any standards, *awesome*, there was much more to do in the suburbs of the Windy City this weekend than the main event. The Public Events stage at the Grand Prix has been busy all weekend, and with the cut for all X-2s on Sunday, today the main focus of many has shifted to the open events. Eight-person drafts, as well as Standard, Extended and Legacy single-elimination events, are running on demand, and demand is high. In addition to these, each day has seen special tournaments that you can only get at an event like a Grand Prix.

Things kicked off on Friday with numerous Grand Prix Trials, but even those who had no interest in Legacy had an event to look forward to - Mega Friday Night Magic attracted 119 players who competed for the chance to win a Magic: The Gathering skinned XBox 360, as well as product prizes and foils. Saturday's marquee event was an Extended tournament for an amazing array of prizes - first place, for instance, had the choice between a play set of Revised dual lands (a full 40), a near-mint Beta [Ancestral Recall](https://web.archive.org/web/20090307000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ancestral+Recall), or a $1000 gift certificate for Pastimes, the primary tournament organizer this weekend. We've pulled the Top 8 decklists from that event, to give you an idea of what the metagame was like - you can find them below.

The Extended action continues today, with 234 players managing to allow for the Daylight Saving Time shift to show up in time to register for the PTQ. With that event still in its infancy, we will have to wait until later to see if those who were successful in the Extended Insanity yesterday are able to repeat, but look for decklists to show up soon on **magicthegathering.com**‘s deck archive.

Other Legacy and Vintage scheduled events have happened or are happening as well, but the attention of many is on the last big event of the weekend - the Public Events Championship. Entering any event this weekend got you event tickets which allowed you to enter into a drawing - the more events you played, the more tickets you got. At 3 p.m. today they're going to draw 14 names from that pool, to go with two lucky players from the Grand Prix yesterday, and those 16 are going to have a draft, followed by single elimination. Anyone who wins at least one match will win a piece of power - a full set of the Power 9 is being given away to the Top 8 from that event! Win one match to win a piece of power - the pressure is on!

#### Kenny Castor
